# Implementation status against work.md

## Expedia via Omkar live recheck — 2026-11-23

Current verification: full `npm test` **642/642**; `npm run build` passes after retrying past the Next.js security reminder; audit reports one moderate Next.js issue, with its available fix outside the declared dependency range.

The locally configured Omkar wrapper returned HTTP 200 for one read-only YHZ–LAX query dated 2026-12-10 (one adult, economy, one-way): 80 flights, zero accepted fares, zero price-only observations, 227 aggregate route/fare rejections; the previous counter did not distinguish those categories. A size-bounded schema-only inspection of the first sampled flight/fare found no currency metadata field and no offset-aware segment timestamps; the formatted fare string was not inspected for currency evidence. No raw values were printed, and this single sample does not establish why every row failed. Added aggregate rejection diagnostics to the adapter without payload fields. Official docs provide no currency parameter; the common Omkar API transport now honors documented quota reset headers when a 429 reports zero remaining. Focused Expedia/provider/transport tests passed 35/35. Direct Expedia partner API credentials and seller verification remain unavailable; Omkar is reachable but not currently producing displayable verified CAD results.

## ACV structured adult multi-room path — 2026-11-23

The ordinary-widget JSON adapter now accepts adult-only multi-room packages when each room is explicitly represented by the provider listing's `r0..rN` parameters. Live widget evidence for two adults/two rooms showed one adult in each room; the per-passenger row total multiplied by passenger count reconciled with the group total for all 20 observed package rows. A direct structured adapter invocation accepted 20/20, retained both room allocations and passed the worker result validator. This was not a durable website/worker UI run. The UI displays the verified room allocation. The path rejects missing/malformed room entries, wrong aggregate adults, children across multiple rooms and mismatched worker evidence. Focused tests pass **40/40**, full `npm test` **641/641**, and production build passes after retrying past the Next.js security reminder. Audit reports one moderate Next.js 16.4.3 vulnerability; no upgrade was applied because the available fix is outside the declared range. Open: child multi-room/infant package support, durable UI evidence, >20 pages, complete flight-segment chronology, seller availability and mandatory charges.

## ACV structured three-child party — 2026-11-22

The ordinary-widget JSON adapter and worker now support up to three exact-age children in one room. Live widget evidence showed a single comma-separated age parameter and one adult plus three child rows across all 20 sampled packages; a fresh structured adapter call returned 20/20 normalized records and passed the worker result validator for ages 7, 12 and 15. Input age order must match request evidence, provider listing age fields, and the explicit room allocation. The fast path remains limited to one page of 20 and no infants; four-or-more-child parties stay on the rendered path. A one-adult/eight-child query was refused by the provider form before submission; the maximum supported count is unknown. Focused tests pass 38/38 and full `npm test` passes 639/639. `npm run build` passes after retrying past the Next.js security reminder. Audit reports one moderate Next.js vulnerability; no upgrade was applied. This was not a durable job/UI run and does not verify checkout, complete segment chronology, extra charges, or seller availability.

## ACV structured child party path — 2026-11-23

The ordinary-widget JSON adapter and worker guard now support an adult party with one exact-age child in one room. Request evidence binds `child-ages` to the submitted age; listing evidence independently checks `non_adult1` and the `r0` adult/child allocation; package pricing must contain adult (`A`) and child (`C`) passenger rows with exact counts and cent totals reconciling through taxes/fees to the group amount. Children and infants remain unsupported on this fast path except one child; other shapes keep the rendered-card path. Focused tests passed 36/36; full `npm test` passed 637/637 after stale ITA Matrix fixture dates were moved into the future. A fresh ordinary-widget run for one adult plus age-7 child returned 20 records, accepted 20/20, and passed the package worker result validator. This was adapter plus validator evidence, not a durable website/UI run: `next dev` stopped on a Next.js security-upgrade prompt before the browser check. `npm run build` passed after retrying past the reminder. `npm audit --audit-level=low` reports one moderate Next.js vulnerability (installed 16.4.3, affected range 16.0.0–16.4.4); the available upgrade is outside the declared dependency range and has not been applied. No names/prices/raw response were printed. Offset-aware complete flight segments, multi-room allocation, exact property/room, checkout availability, local charges and seller total remain open. See [the discovery note](docs/acv-structured-package-endpoint-discovery-2026-11-14.md).

## ACV structured package adapter hardening — 2026-11-14

The adult-only, 20-record Air Canada Vacations widget JSON path now binds hotel `destinationId` to the request's `dest` field, uses the observed widget economy request code `Y` and itinerary cabin code `Y`, interprets `currentEnd` as an inclusive zero-based index, and recognizes the observed adult pax marker `A` only when exact adult counts and totals reconcile. The durable worker rechecks destination evidence, adult pax rows, itinerary/hotel joins, dates, totals and freshness. Regression fixtures cover wrong destination, cabin, inclusive page metadata, child pax markers and worker tampering. A fresh local website-to-worker run completed in structured mode: 20 provider records, 20 accepted/rendered, 78 total listings reported, additional results beyond the bounded first page, CAD visible, no worker error; persisted job duration 24.955 seconds. This is one live observation, not a latency commitment. Focused tests passed 34/34. Child/infant and 40-result queries still use rendered listing parsing. ACV itinerary entries remain journey summaries, not a timezone-validated complete segment chain. Multi-room allocation, exact address/room, checkout availability, all local charges and final seller-confirmed totals remain open. See [the discovery note](docs/acv-structured-package-endpoint-discovery-2026-11-14.md).

## Google Flights date-grid path — 2026-11-14

Implemented the normal-UI `GetCalendarPicker` response path through `lib/google-calendar.mjs`, calendar dispatch, worker validation and `app/FareCalendar.js`. Scope is one adult in economy, one exact airport pair, no active filters, for a one-way query or a fixed-return round trip. The response remains a price-only observation; the UI search action preserves the exact trip type and return date. The parser and worker bind provider, CAD, route, dates, fixed return, source URL, price-only disclosure and offset-aware timestamp. A single grid is reused across up to 31 dates while logical query budget and wire-call coverage are kept separate. The adapter remains opt-in (`TRAVEL_GOOGLE_CALENDAR_RPC=1`).

Current bounded live evidence: ordinary YHZ–LAX round trip, departures Dec 1–8, 2026, fixed return Dec 8, one adult/economy. The adapter returned 8/8 requested date rows with amounts and visibly confirmed CAD. No itinerary, fare rules, baggage, seller availability or final charges were returned. A separate one-way query failed closed with numeric marker 4; no error meaning is inferred. API Anything's `priceCalendar` error 13 is a separate, still-unusable operation.

Verification: focused parser/calendar/worker suites **61/61**; `TMPDIR=/dev/shm npm test` **631/631**; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit` reports 0 vulnerabilities. Fixed-return round-trip behavior now has live evidence; one-way live behavior, endpoint stability, provider scope and all broader §17 criteria remain open. See [dated evidence](docs/reverse-endpoint-progressive-results-2026-11-14.md#google-flights-date-grid-integration--2026-11-14), [endpoint inventory](docs/reverse-endpoint-implementation.md), and [all-source implementation plan](docs/reverse-endpoint-full-implementation-plan.md).

## ACV structured suggestion response bound — 2026-11-14

`lib/vacation-suggestions.mjs` rejects an advertised or decoded `/geo-insight/suggestions` response above 1 MB and parses JSON only after the decoded-byte check. Exact term matching and the existing 20-option cap remain. A process-local exact-query cache holds validated location suggestions for five minutes (100-entry cap); it keys destination results by selected origin too and preserves the initial observation time on cache hits. Focused suggestion tests pass 10/10 including cache eviction; final-tree `npm test` passes **619/619** (89.6s). `npm run build` and `npm audit --audit-level=low` (0 vulnerabilities) passed after implementation changes. No live provider call was made. Playwright may buffer bytes before application inspection; direct unauthenticated ACV access previously returned 401, and an approved server-auth contract is not established. Package-listing pages still lack a uniform cap. This narrows transport/repeat-query costs but does not complete the broader reverse-endpoint goal or any additional work.md §17 criterion. [Evidence](docs/acv-suggestion-response-bound-2026-11-14.md).

## Reverse-endpoint fan-out and progressive flight results — 2026-11-14

Normal flight search publishes fresh provider results progressively through durable job progress, with exact-route/source validation and a job-store persistence timestamp. All-source fan-out is bounded to two concurrent adapters by default and at most four; unsupported party shapes remain provider-specific statuses. Shared HTTP requests report sanitized per-provider latency histograms and roll nested provider/calendar measurements into enclosing operations. Google Flights native RPC calls separately report a per-query fixed-bucket latency histogram; it does not cover browser startup, other browser sources, or cross-job p95. Booking.com Demand API uses shared durable pacing/cooldown, preserves Retry-After without POST retries, and caps decoded JSON bodies at 2 MB. Ignav/Expedia/Skyscanner/Omkar JSON and Air Canada NDC XML responses have explicit decoded-body caps. ITA Matrix API response bodies are streamed under 5 MB, with the same decoded batch cap enforced for API and browser parsing. ACV currency-terms HTML uses a 2 MB decoded-body cap; widget package/location operations and Booking.com public-page searches now share durable source pacing and auth/rate cooldowns, though browser package/Booking pages still lack uniform byte limits. Latest verification: Google native suite passed 11/11; Booking/ACV widget suites 43/43; full `npm test` passed **616/616**, `npm run build` passed, and `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. The prior production streaming browser check measured 387 ms; the latest local dev-stack fixture recheck measured **454 ms** from persistence to UI with three polls, provider calls intercepted, zero browser errors, and zero external requests. No live inventory was used in those checks. User-reported source permission is acknowledged; endpoint/account scope, partner credentials, source-specific rate records, live endpoint validation, durable cross-job telemetry and end-to-end p95 remain open. See [implementation evidence](docs/reverse-endpoint-progressive-results-2026-11-14.md) and [endpoint inventory](docs/reverse-endpoint-implementation.md).

**Open reverse-endpoint feasibility:** A bundled API Anything Google `priceCalendar` call for YYZ–LHR (2026-12-01), with CAD/en-CA/CA requested, returned batchexecute internal error 13 on its initial call and one indicated-safe retry; no price/currency evidence was returned, so it remains unwired. An unauthenticated direct Air Canada Vacations suggestion request returned 401, while the ordinary widget lookup succeeded with one matching YHZ result and an Authorization header present. No token value was captured or saved. The direct call activated the shared provider cooldown; wait for it to expire before any source call. The broader reverse-endpoint goal remains open; see the [dated endpoint checks](docs/reverse-endpoint-progressive-results-2026-11-14.md#reverse-endpoint-feasibility-checks--2026-11-14).

## P13 mobile touch target follow-up — 2026-11-14

Set the frequently used location-type selects, filter reset, worker-status action and advanced-filter fields to a 44px minimum. `check:responsive-ui` now asserts the designated compact controls meet 44px at 320px. Fresh build browser checks passed: 17 designated controls at 44px+, all 66 sampled initial-page targets at 44px+, no overflow at 320/375/768px or enlarged text spacing; theme contrast remains 5.27:1 dark / 5.53:1 light, and accessibility remains at 113 labeled controls. P13 still requires dynamic-state, screen-reader and physical-device validation. [Audit report](docs/frontend-accessibility-audit-2026-11-14.md).

## P13 semantic theme tokens — 2026-11-14

Centralized the main dark/light surfaces, text, border, accent and status palette in 48 referenced CSS custom properties in `app/globals.css`. The contrast browser check now verifies required token values resolve and that background/text change by theme. The fresh production build passed; fresh-build contrast passed at 5.27:1 dark and 5.53:1 light across 164 sampled text nodes per theme; accessibility check passed with 113 named controls; responsive check passed at 320/375/768px and enlarged text spacing. Full `npm test` passed 599/599 using temporary isolated API Anything directories; `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. A test-isolation defect was also corrected: `lib/booking-browser.mjs` now respects `TRAVEL_API_ANYTHING_HOME`. The subsequent touch-target update raised all 66 sampled initial-page controls to at least 44px at 320px. ## P13 frontend accessibility and implementation audit — 2026-11-14

Current production-build Chromium checks passed: 113 named controls with no broken references, keyboard disclosure and dynamic airline suggestion behavior, sampled dark/light contrast minimum 5.27:1, and responsive text reflow without horizontal overflow at 320/375/768px. Of 66 visible initial-page targets measured at 320px, 12 are under 44px but all satisfy the sampled 24px WCAG AA floor. The initial local production page transferred ~192 KB compressed JS; a single loopback load is not field performance evidence. `impeccable detect` returned one Arial typography warning, assessed as a false positive for product-specific integrity. Score 14/20 (Good), with tokenized theming and touch comfort as P2 follow-ups. No code change was made. This does not close P13 or §17: assistive technology, dynamic states, physical devices, and full WCAG conformance remain unverified. [Detailed audit](docs/frontend-accessibility-audit-2026-11-14.md).

## P12 encrypted-backup key-reader hardening — 2026-11-14 17:12 UTC

Backup keys are opened with no-follow semantics, ownership/type/mode are checked on the descriptor, and reads are capped at 129 bytes. Encrypted archives are opened once without following symlinks and decrypted from that same descriptor. Regressions cover oversized/symlinked keys and a symlinked archive. Focused backup suite 3/3, isolated recovery drill, full suite 599/599 (84.3s), isolated production build and zero-vulnerability audit passed. `.env.local` is mode `0600`. This improves local key handling but does not provide scheduled/offsite copies, escrow, production restore, external monitoring or deployment security. [Evidence](docs/encrypted-job-backups-2026-11-14.md).

Inspection showed high-frequency `get()` polls forced the full retention cleanup and executed two unconditional expiry updates. `get()` now uses the cleanup throttle, removes the specifically requested expired terminal job immediately, and runs lease/deadline updates only when the selected row requires them. Seller-offer lookup uses the retention cutoff rather than a forced sweep.
- Red/green regression: polling a recent job no longer deletes an unrelated expired row; requesting that expired row still removes it immediately. `node --test tests/job-store.test.mjs` passed 18/18; focused source/error tests passed 29/29; final `npm test` passed 541/541. SQLite busy/locked errors now return a generic HTTP 503 with a one-second `Retry-After`, not HTTP 400 or raw DB text. The isolated production build passed after all final changes. The observed lock response is the motivating incident; this change lowers write contention but is not a proof of zero future SQLite contention.
- `npm audit` found moderate advisories in Hono 4.13.14 through the API Anything MCP SDK. `npm audit fix` moved the compatible lockfile resolution to Hono 4.13.18 and reported zero vulnerabilities. Post-update `npm audit --audit-level=low` found zero vulnerabilities; isolated production build and final full test suite passed. ## P9 shared Matrix API response cache — 2026-11-08

- Successful normalized ITA Matrix API responses now use the shared SQLite database `.cache/jobs/search.sqlite` with a five-minute TTL. SHA-256 cache keys isolate validated request content and API key without persisting the raw key. The cache permits at most 100 entries and 1 MB per response, removes expired/corrupt rows, and labels reused sources `cached: true`. The browser-based Matrix family/infant transport returns before the API cache and remains uncached.

## P2 age-only round-trip boundary correction — 2026-11-07

## P4 Booking.com city verification before browser search — 2026-11-07

- The Booking.com browser path always resolves a canonical city before opening its source, including standalone typed-city searches without an airport/city ID. It sends the resolved city and ID to the browser session and returns that verified destination in the result. Resolver errors/city failures stop before any Booking.com session and produce `unverified_destination`; `both` sources do not retry Booking.com when shared destination resolution failed.
- Regression test was red before the fix (resolver not called; search session started even when resolver was unavailable). `node --test tests/hotel-search.test.mjs` passed 11/11 after the fix. Full `npm test` passed 444/444 (75.5s), build passed, audit found zero vulnerabilities. Rebuilt-loopback `check:hotel-occupancy` verified exact two-room age assignments and explicit simultaneous-availability caveat with provider/job requests intercepted and zero browser errors.
- Root returned HTTP 200 and `/api/health` returned `ok` at `2026-11-07T16:24:31Z` (queue 0, one live worker, no expired leases/deadlines; three stale and three stopped historical records). No live hotel query was run. P4 remains **Partial** pending live child multi-room rates, exact property/rate matching, simultaneous inventory, complete mandatory fees and seller checkout confirmation.

## P6 Air Canada Vacations lap-infant estimate support — 2026-11-07

- `app/VacationSearch.js` collects DOB only in browser state and uses `ageOnDate` for departure and return. The job receives derived ages and `seating: "lap"`, never the DOB; `lib/vacation-query.mjs` enforces an exact three-field infant schema so a caller cannot persist a birth date. Eligibility is limited to ages 0–1 on both trip dates, at most one lap infant per adult, one room when minors are present, and nine total travelers. Seated infants and age-crossing trips fail closed.

Result cards remain estimates because the provider receives only the count, not DOB. Current official terms [updated October 2026](https://vacations.aircanada.com/en/terms-conditions) require an accurate DOB at booking, set one lap infant per accompanying adult aged 16+, and require child-package pricing if the child turns two by the return flight. ## Local SQLite job-database recovery — 2026-11-07

- Added a documented CLI: `npm run jobs:database -- backup <new-file>`, `verify <file>`, and `restore <verified-backup> <new-file>`. Backup makes a transaction-consistent SQLite snapshot in WAL mode with `VACUUM INTO`, then checks integrity, foreign keys and required job schema. Final files are `0600`; newly created parent directories are `0700`; existing destinations are never replaced. CLI output excludes database contents.
- Restore writes only to a new destination and marks queued/running/paused jobs interrupted, clears lease tokens and hotel challenge state, and closes outstanding browser interactions. This prevents automatic provider-call replay or stale CAPTCHA revival on worker startup; completed result observations are retained. README documents that both website and worker must stop before switching `TRAVEL_JOB_DB`, and that backup files need encrypted storage/transfer because permissions alone do not encrypt them.
- `node --test tests/job-database-backup.test.mjs`: 2/2 passed. Coverage includes an open WAL database, completed and queued jobs, active hotel challenge plus pending interaction, restore result, no automatic claim, sensitive-output exclusion, private file mode, no-overwrite, same-path refusal and corrupt database rejection.
- Current verification after the change: `npm test` 429/429 (131.7 seconds); `npm run build` passed; `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. At `2026-11-07T14:08:57Z`, loopback root and `/api/health` returned HTTP 200; health `ok`, queue 0, one live worker, zero expired leases/deadlines, three stale and three stopped historical worker records. No provider fare search was made for this change.
- P12 / §17 Worker health remains **Partial**. `npm run check:job-recovery` now passes a disposable-database backup/restore drill, but no production-data restore drill, scheduled backup, encrypted/offsite recovery, external alerting, multi-user authentication, general abuse controls, credential encryption/rotation, or public security review exists. Keep the app local-only. Next actions: establish protected backup storage/retention and an authorized operational restore-drill dataset; add deployment-appropriate monitoring and identity before reconsidering exposure.

## Durable job-start abuse limits — 2026-11-07

- The SQLite job store now limits each owner capability to five active jobs and 10 new starts per rolling minute, with a 20-active-job and 30-starts-per-minute service-wide ceiling. The rolling event table is persisted in the same job database and pruned during new submissions. Existing request-key retries do not consume quota; expired-window retries become available at the exact boundary. `POST /api/search/jobs` returns HTTP 429 and a `Retry-After` header for rolling-window limits.
- Test-first coverage: `tests/job-store.test.mjs` verifies owner/global caps, idempotency, completion release, rolling-window expiry and restart persistence; `tests/search-jobs-route.test.mjs` verifies route statuses, owner isolation and `Retry-After`. Focused tests passed 21/21. The first full run had one regional calendar worker deadline failure under suite load; that test passed alone and the next full run passed 427/427. `npm run build` passed and `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. Rebuilt-site `check:search-settings` and `check:route-settings` passed with provider work intercepted.
- After restarting both the built website and worker, loopback at `2026-11-07T13:30:23.662Z`: root HTTP 200; `/api/health` `ok`, queue depth 0, one live worker, zero expired leases/deadlines, three stale and three stopped historical worker rows. This limits job starts through the shared SQLite database, not every API route. Owner capabilities are not user authentication; public multi-instance service still needs shared identity and quotas, alerting, backup/restore and security review. Keep app local-only.

## Europe airport geography aligned with UN M49 — 2026-11-06

- Europe airport candidates now derive from the official live UNSD M49 Europe (region 150) country/area set mapped through Wikidata ISO entities, followed by an explicit airport-level coordinate convention (25°W–45°E, 34°N–72°N). This keeps airport inclusion from depending only on a country’s continent claim. The approximate boundary excludes known out-of-box airports and withholds airports without valid coordinates; both categories and up to 100 codes per category now flow through flight and calendar coverage. Merged policy version: `UNSD-M49-2026-10+TH-2026-11+airport-box-v1+dual-directory-v2`.
- Popularity ordering remains based on Wikidata passenger observations; the top three candidates are reported and excluded from fare selection. Candidates without traffic evidence follow alphabetically. Airport-class subclasses are included, OurAirports/Wikidata records merge by IATA code, resolver cache moved to `regions-v10`, and source query caps/saturated batches remain explicitly disclosed.

Focused geography/calendar/provider tests passed 60/60; `npm run check:region-coverage`, `check:route-settings`, `check:calendar-ranges`, and `check:search-settings` passed against the rebuilt loopback site with fare/job work intercepted. Full `npm test` passed 414/414; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities.
- Read-only live Europe resolution at 2026-11-06T22:01:45Z used a fresh Wikidata result and cached OurAirports snapshot: 51 country/area entries, 100 selected airports, 743 known resolver candidates beyond that selection, top exclusions LHR/CDG/MAD, first selected FRA, 268 known airports outside the coordinate boundary and zero without coordinates. ## Local worker health endpoint added — 2026-11-06

- Added loopback-only `GET /api/health`, backed by aggregate SQLite job counts and worker heartbeat rows. It exposes queue depth by product/status, oldest queued time, live/stale/stopped worker counts, expired lease/deadline counts, and observation time. It returns no job IDs, user queries, results, tokens, provider payloads or credentials; it is not an authenticated public deployment health service.
- The worker writes a heartbeat on each poll and marks itself stopped on orderly shutdown. A running worker is considered live only when its heartbeat is no more than five seconds old. Health reports `degraded` when queued work has no live worker or expired work is present.
- Regression tests cover healthy and degraded snapshots, private-data omission, and cross-origin/non-local refusal. Live loopback probe at `2026-11-06T19:48:28.022Z` returned HTTP 200, `status: ok`, queue depth 0, one live worker, zero stale workers, zero expired leases and zero expired deadlines.
- Full `npm test` passed 404/404; `npm run build` passed and `npm audit --audit-level=low` found 0 vulnerabilities. After restart, Chromium `check:search-settings` and `check:calendar-source` also passed against the built site with provider calls intercepted. Website and worker remain local-only at `127.0.0.1:3000`.
- P12 remains partial: aggregate local health is not alerting, user authentication, general abuse quota/rate limiting, credential encryption/rotation, backup/restore verification, or a production deployment security review. Do not expose the app publicly until those gates pass.

## Latest verified continuation — job ownership and hotel-source audit — 2026-11-06

- The durable job API now requires a UUID v4 capability generated per job. Only its SHA-256 digest is persisted; constant-time ownership checks guard job status and control actions, deletion and Ignav seller-link lookup. The UI stores the capability beside the job ID in tab-scoped session storage and restores it after reload. Legacy active records without an owner digest are cancelled at migration.
- This narrows local job access but is not account authentication or public deployment security. No user accounts, revocation, per-user quotas, general API rate limits, alerting or operational monitoring are established; remaining provider routes rely on same-origin loopback protection. The app must remain local-only.

## Integration plan execution: native Google calendar fallback enabled — 2026-11-05

- Implemented `lib/google-native.mjs` and wired Google economy/non-infant round trips through capabilities, exact search, calendar validation/dispatch/checkpoints, worker and refresh UI/API. Native access has its own `TRAVEL_GOOGLE_ROUND_TRIP=1` setting, enabled in local `.env.local`; the existing one-way operation retains its limits. Chromium is an explicit dependency and external server package.
- Native flow retains the complete canonical party while sending source fare counts only. It examines at most three eligible outbounds, at most 80 returned rows per direction list, and stops at the first complete matching return. Each query has a 45-second overall deadline and a 12-fare-RPC request bound enforced before dispatch. Required source party, CAD, both directions, segment identities, seller flight references and IANA/UTC chronology must agree; no seller continuation/order is automated. Phone-only pricing is explicit.
- Fixed two actual integration failures: browser navigation could retire a protocol response before capture, and calendars were omitting filters from exact provider queries. Capture now preserves browser response data before navigation and accepts complete streamed direction frames only when they contain an eligible direction; final seller quotes require the complete selected-trip response. Exact calendar filters now reach provider selection and the final shared filters. Unrecognized empty source frames are incomplete, never proof of no inventory or complete coverage.

A real isolated all-compatible regional worker left queued and used exactly four slots: Ignav HTTP 200 twice; Google produced a valid complete quote on one date and timed out on another, with incomplete coverage retained. After fixing filter propagation, a real two-slot Google regional worker produced a matching CAD 19,356 group quote for Dec 18–28. Private captures stay ignored under `.cache/google-discovery/`.

A live direct refresh returned four fresh quotes matching the original itinerary and party, including a changed observed price. Google seller-site final price and acceptance remain unverified.
- Expedia’s official two-segment listings contract is implemented for exact adult/child-age group round trips and exact-flight-only refresh. Normalization requires both linked directions, passenger-category counts, matching cabin and exact flight/date evidence; the split-ticket flag remains visible. Flexible child calendars stay excluded without DOB support. Expedia partner credentials are absent, so no live quote is claimed.

## Audit correctness fixes — 2026-10-31

Eight audit defects were corrected with regression coverage: unsafe money parsing; contradictory Omkar passenger-price scope; silently ignored infant/party aliases; Matrix cabin inferred from the request; connection-summary filter bypass; Matrix native-fetch pacing bypass; undefined request-limit variable on separate-ticket refusal; and frontend sort override. Existing age-category and package-widget fixes were retained. Fully normalized Matrix results now require actual returned cabin evidence; missing evidence stays price-only and contradictory evidence is excluded. ## Identified segment-date guard follow-up — 2026-10-31

Current code review found a fare-category gap: identified travelers were checked against all returned local segment dates, while age-only count queries were checked only against the requested departure and return dates. An age-11 child on an overnight outbound could therefore receive an offer even though Ignav classifies age 12+ as adult and the segment arrival date was unknown from the count query.

`lib/ignav.mjs` now runs the age-only round-trip boundary guard across every observed departure and arrival date on the outbound and inbound segments. Offers that may cross age 12 without a DOB are withheld; the result limitations disclose this. Added a regression that first failed for an age-11 overnight arrival, confirms the offer is withheld, and confirms age 10 and same-day age-11 travel remain eligible for normalization. Full suite: 279 passed; production build passed; `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. These are fixture tests; no fare request or booking was made for this change.

The same-day/overnight rule is an eligibility-category safeguard, not proof of airline fare eligibility. Long-trip age-only parties are conservatively rejected by the earlier duration rule. Carrier fare basis, operating-carrier requirements, and seller acceptance remain open. Priority 2 remains partial.

Package status: the full UI runner again failed before package submission during origin lookup. Earlier direct live checks show the widget sometimes overwrites typed text with its Toronto default. Code now checks typed text and retries once. That has not yielded a reliable full live package result. Continue from this blocker only after inspecting the current widget request/input timing; do not repeat a package search until both exact location selections succeed.

## Longer-trip age boundary and package recheck — 2026-10-31

Reviewing priority 2 found `validateIgnavRoundTripAge` checked only age 11. An age-10 count-only party on a two-year return trip could previously reach the provider with an unsupported assumption about return category. The shared guard now checks every supplied child age below 12 against the trip length, using a conservative 365-day lower bound and requiring DOB when crossing age 12 is possible. It may request DOB near a boundary rather than guess the birthday. Short age-10 trips and existing adult-category ages remain supported. Identified parties retain their exact DOB validation.

A regression first failed on the long-trip age-10 case. It now passes, and a direct Ignav adapter assertion verifies zero network requests for that ambiguous party. Full suite: 278 passed; focused traveler suite: 7 passed; production build passed; npm audit reported zero vulnerabilities. Existing shared callers inherit the guard; no provider fare call or booking occurred. Carrier-specific actual fare eligibility remains unverified. Continue resolving live package initialization and other independent work.md requirements; do not mark overall acceptance complete.

A controlled live initialized-widget comparison successfully typed `YHZ` and then the full Halifax label; both produced HTTP 200 suggestions containing the exact Halifax origin, which was selected. ## Origin reselection: readiness experiment — 2026-10-31

A fresh real-provider probe reproduced the origin reselection failure. Before typing, the visible origin input was empty. After typing the full Halifax label and waiting 15 seconds for its exact suggestion, the input contained `Toronto Pearson Intl. Airport (YYZ)` and no matching suggestion response was observed. The provider's late initialization can overwrite entered text; visible input alone is not reliable readiness evidence.

A second controlled probe changed only readiness: it waited until the origin input held a nonempty provider default before calling the existing selector with the full Halifax label. That probe still timed out waiting for the exact Halifax suggestion. Therefore waiting for a nonempty default alone is NOT an established fix. ## Interrupted live package attempt — 2026-10-31

A full website search attempt failed before job submission. The API returned `provider_suggestion_timeout` for the origin. A redacted Playwright probe then found the decisive signal: the actual widget request was `query=YHZToronto Pearson Intl. Airport (YYZ)` with `journeyType=origin`, rather than the requested exact term `YHZ`. This indicates the provider page retained/appended its preset Toronto label despite the adapter's Ctrl+A/Backspace sequence. Earlier lookups did succeed, so this is an intermittent widget-state issue. No HTTP refusal was seen in this failed attempt; it is premature to attribute it to throttling.

This is stronger evidence than the prior generic timeout. The next fix should make the location selection wait for the input to equal the intended term and inspect the resulting request query before accepting a suggestion. If the widget retains a preset, use its ordinary visible clear control or reinitialise the widget session before typing; don't manually edit the readonly date fields or bypass provider controls. Keep a regression for concatenated text and rerun the same UI probe before attempting another fare search. The previous worker and app use a separate audit DB at `/tmp/travel-package-audit-20261031/jobs.sqlite`; no package job was submitted. The app and worker launched for this attempt may still be running after interruption; check their process/session state and stop them after inspection. Official Ignav documentation describes these counts as optional fare fields, not a checkout guarantee ([response format](https://ignav.com/docs/response-format)). Focused Ignav tests pass; `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` passed 220 tests, `npm run build` passed, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. Controlled Chromium rendered a present carry-on count, an absent checked-bag count, and the unitemized-charges warning with zero page errors. No live seller or baggage check was made.

Seller handoff coverage is now computed against Ignav's returned itinerary. One-way/round-trip `outbound`/`inbound` coverage and flexible-search zero-based leg indexes are both supported. The adapter reports `complete` only when known, non-overlapping purchase options with usable links cover every returned itinerary leg; it reports `partial` for missing-link/leg coverage and `unknown` if the itinerary or coverage cannot be mapped. The API retrieves the selected offer from durable search results and compares every flight segment with the provider-returned itinerary. If they differ, it suppresses seller links; if the original offer cannot be recovered, the match stays unknown. The UI displays both match and coverage status. `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` passed 219 tests; `npm run build` passed; `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. Controlled Chromium checked matched and mismatched responses: one link shown for the matching itinerary, zero links for a mismatch, and zero page errors. The existing home lockfile and Node module-type warnings remain. No live seller request was made. Exact seller availability, baggage, mandatory charges and checkout total remain unverified.

Booking-link responses now retain a valid supplied top-level observation timestamp, with its scope explicitly unspecified in the UI; missing or invalid timestamps remain unknown. An isolated `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` rerun passed 213 tests after the concurrent build/full-suite run timed out an existing hotel-worker test. Build passed before the final wording clarification, and `npm audit` found zero vulnerabilities. No live seller or browser verification was made.

Seller handoff options now display validated direction/index coverage, per-option price scope, and link specificity; unknown coverage remains unknown. Ignav itinerary price is labeled as a provider quote that may be the search quote. The full suite passed 212 tests, build passed with the existing home lockfile warning, and audit found zero vulnerabilities. Live seller and browser verification remain open.

The identified-traveler form now clears optional seating and guardian fields correctly; validation rejects infant seating when the traveler is at least two on all supplied dates. Calendar dates affected by that conflict are marked unsupported without fare requests. After correcting the calendar expectation and isolating an unintended live Matrix call in provider tests, the full suite passed 211 tests; build passed and audit found zero vulnerabilities. No live fare or browser check was made for this change. Carrier-specific eligibility and age-category-changing trips remain open.

Current checks: `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2` passed 211 tests, `npm run build` passed, and `npm audit` reported zero vulnerabilities. The build retains the home lockfile warning and tests emit the existing module-type warning. These checks do not establish live fares or carrier eligibility.

Historical verification at the earlier multi-room Demand API integration checkpoint (512 tests; superseded by the 2026-11-08 515-test checkpoint above): `npm test` passed 512/512 in 79.96 seconds, `npm run build` passed, `npm audit --audit-level=low` found zero vulnerabilities, rebuilt-loopback `check:flight-hotel-demand` passed with all provider traffic intercepted and zero browser errors, and `npm run check:local-stack` passed graceful shutdown with an available worker and zero provider searches. The historical Expedia URL-builder and YHZ–LAX Matrix observations below describe earlier dated checks; they remain non-seller-verified. See the dated evidence in `HANDOFF.md` and the supporting notes under `docs/`.

### Historical audit log

The dated notes below record earlier milestones and are superseded by the current audit snapshot and §17 table above where test counts or remaining-work statements differ.

2026-10-29 audit continuation: Wikidata region discovery now orders country/airport candidates, reports up to 300 selected countries plus the first known omitted country, and reports the first known airport omitted by its 10-airport resolver cap. Region search carries resolver omissions, unsearched selected pairs, and known pair combinations involving airports outside the selected limits. The UI renders country coverage and an omitted-pairs disclosure. Controlled Chromium verified those details with a mocked result and zero page errors. Regression tests validate caps and propagation; they do not validate live taxonomy or the requested geographic conventions. `npm test -- --test-concurrency=2`: 189 passed; `npm run build`: passed with the existing home lockfile warning; `npm audit`: zero vulnerabilities. No live region query was made. All 28 region conventions, transcontinental boundaries, any countries beyond the 300 limit, airports beyond the first known omission, and comprehensive airport coverage remain open.

2026-10-29 local-only gate: `dev` and `start` scripts now bind Next.js to `127.0.0.1` instead of its default all-interface host. A production server check confirmed a loopback-only listener and HTTP 200, then stopped the server. This reduces LAN exposure; deployment authentication, per-user job ownership, abuse protection, monitoring and recovery gates remain incomplete. Final checks after this change: 189 tests passed with `--test-concurrency=2`, build passed, audit reported zero vulnerabilities.

Earlier audit continuation: Expedia journey duration now uses offset-aware first departure and last arrival, including layovers, and rejects missing offsets or invalid leg chronology. The full 169-test suite, production build and npm audit passed; the final timestamp-range guard was followed by the three Expedia tests. The build retains the home-directory lockfile warning. The wider audit and remaining acceptance criteria below remain open.

Traveler boundary continuation: unsupported infant and identity inputs are rejected across direct flight adapters and shared validation, preventing an adult-only quote for an explicitly larger party. Full suite: 172 passed; build passed with the same warning; npm audit found zero vulnerabilities. Infant pricing and end-to-end identity support remain open.

## Airport popularity selection and region search budget — 2026-10-31

Initial Europe popularity-ranking attempt (superseded by the live-verified continuation below): the UI default was 10 and resolver data sorted by P3872, but a stale cache preserved alphabetic results and the UI range text was inaccurate. The subsequent live-verified implementation below replaces that result path and documents its current data limits.

Added fixture regressions covering popularity sort, default 10, custom count, and rejection above the 20-airport bound before external calls. Focused tests passed (15/15). A live Europe resolver attempt timed out after 27.2s, so live candidate ordering and fare results remain unverified. Continental Europe still uses a documented approximate coordinate box atop M49 country membership; countries/territories, airport geocodes, and Wikidata traffic evidence may be incomplete. Do not claim comprehensive coverage. Full suite, production build, npm audit, and browser/API verification are still required for this code change.

Verification checkpoint for airport ranking/budget UI: full suite 297/297 passed, production build passed, `npm audit` found 0 vulnerabilities. Live Europe resolution timed out after 27.2s, so real ranking and fare-search coverage are not verified. No browser fare search or booking occurred. Next: rerun live resolution when Wikidata/UN M49 responds, then verify one website query and its selected/searched airport coverage.

Rendered website check: the built site ran locally at port 3457. Chromium observed airport-limit min 4, max 20, default 10; changing it to 14 persisted and the UI recalculated two-region coverage as 196 pairs. The temporary server/browser were stopped. This verifies the visible control only; it is not live airport-ranking or fare-search evidence.

## Europe airport popularity: live implementation verification — 2026-10-31

Replaced the slow all-country passenger aggregation for Europe with a live Wikidata query over countries classified as part of Europe, filtering airport coordinates to the documented approximate continental box. Airport classes include subclasses, so major hubs such as LHR and CDG are no longer dropped. Direct Wikidata P3872 traffic observations are sorted by highest recorded passenger figure; duplicate IATA codes retain their highest figure. Raw observations are capped at 1,000; the resolver returns at most 100 ranked airports and discloses further candidates. Airports without P3872 traffic or coordinates are excluded. Cache namespace `.cache/regions-v7` prevents earlier alphabetic cached results from overriding the new policy.

Live `resolveRegion('Europe')` succeeded in 5.1s. It returned 100 candidates and 247 additional ranked airport codes. The first ten were IST, LHR, CDG, AMS, MAD, FRA, FCO, SAW, BCN, MUC, ordered by recorded passenger counts. These values may be historical and are not current annual traffic verification. The Q46 country assignment and 25°W–45°E / 34°N–72°N box are approximate; transcontinental countries remain possible, airport records are incomplete, and missing traffic-data airports are not searched.

Chromium confirmed the rendered control defaults to 10, supports 4–20, recalculates pair count at 14 to 196, and submits `airportLimit: 14` through the website to the search-job API (API intercepted; no fare search was run). Full test suite passed 297/297, production build passed, and npm audit found 0 vulnerabilities. This covers the requested Europe ranking and configurable airport count; it does not complete the broader work.md acceptance goals or verify supplier fares.

## Main search settings exposed — 2026-10-31

The main flight form now lets the user select one backend-supported source or all sources; the selected source is sent in the search-job request instead of hardcoding `all`. Options mirror the provider router: Google Flights, Ignav, Expedia partner API, Skyscanner, Air Canada NDC, ITA Matrix, and Expedia via Omkar. Help text notes unsupported/unconfigured statuses and that Fare Calendar uses its own source. “More search settings” is expanded by default, making existing stop, group budget (CAD), airline include/exclude, layover country/time filters, and sorting visible without opening a disclosure.

Chromium submitted the selection and verified the intercepted API payload held `source: ita-matrix`, `maxStops: 1`, `maxPrice: 1200`, and `sort: duration`. This was a UI-to-API boundary check only; it did not run a provider or prove any source is configured. Build passed, all 297 tests passed, and npm audit reported zero vulnerabilities. Broader work.md items remain open.

Reproducible UI verification is available through `scripts/check-search-settings-ui.mjs` / `npm run check:search-settings`. With the local production site running, it checks default source `all`, advanced settings initially visible, airport count range 4–20/default 10, and that a custom source plus stop/budget/sort settings reach the search-job API. It intercepts airport and search-job responses and never makes a supplier request. Verified on the built site with ITA Matrix, one stop, CAD 1,200, and duration sort.

The airport-limit helper text now accurately says regional traffic coverage varies and some airports without traffic records may be omitted; this reflects the Europe resolver's traffic-only candidates. The source selector’s support/caveat text is associated via `aria-describedby`. Final production build and the repeatable settings browser check passed.

### P3 alternative route search expansion — 2026-10-31

The validated comparison now accepts a `routeSearchLimit` from 0 to 20, default 8. Separate-ticket UI exposes 0, 4, 8, 12, or 20 additional airport-pair searches; the independent request budget can reduce the selected amount after baseline and exact ticket-block work. Exact searched and unsearched pairs remain in coverage, and route discovery explicitly stays non-exhaustive. Fresh alternative display increased from four to eight matching offers. Added regression coverage for limit validation, eight-pair default, explicit four-pair behavior, selected exact round-trip/party query preservation, budget clipping, and display cap. Chromium against the built local website intercepted every API response/request and verified default 8, selected 12, visible airport availability, and submission of `routeSearchLimit: 12`; no supplier was contacted. Focused checks passed 38 tests. After member-source changes, the full suite passed 299/299, `npm run build` passed, and `npm audit` found 0 vulnerabilities. An initial suite/build concurrency attempt hit a 12-second official-hotel-worker timeout; rerunning the suite alone passed.

P3 remains partial: no live expanded fare search/seller handoff evidence; resolver and providers can omit routes; ground transfer, baggage, mandatory costs, simultaneous inventory, and ticket issuance remain unverified. A user-visible cap cannot expand the upstream airport resolver's coverage.

### P4 hotel comparison and P5 member/promotion checks — 2026-10-31

Current Flight + Hotel code review: exact flight-derived check-in/out dates and DOB-at-check-in ages are required; subtotal matching requires a fresh same-property/served-city/airport match, exact room occupancy/allocation and CAD amounts. Direct-site multi-room quotes are summed only when every room quote is fresh and matches its allocation, and simultaneous inventory remains explicitly unverified. UI calls this an observed subtotal and warns mandatory taxes/fees may be additional. Booking.com browser multi-room child occupancy is refused; Demand API credentials and a normalized provider offer are absent. P4 stays **Partial** because live multi-room/child availability, simultaneous room inventory, and comparable all-in totals have no source evidence.

Improved `member-offers.mjs` source matching and summaries from current official pages. Live public checks at `2026-10-31` found CAA Atlantic's published hotel savings (5–10% listed chains, 15% Rodd, other regional offers), Costco Travel's Canadian resident member requirement and 2% Executive reward after qualifying travel (up to CA$1,250, subject to exclusions), and Visa's up-to-8% / USD 230 cap Booking.com Travel Credits for future stays with eligible Visa Infinite and a free Booking.com account. The public Visa terms also require eligible Canadian-issued Infinite/Infinite+ cards for benefits generally, while the Booking.com offer's exact trip eligibility is not supplied in the page summary. The UI only provides source information; member/card credentials, trip-specific property/rate eligibility, full comparable pricing, and current offer terms are not verified. Rewards are not deducted from the observed quote. Targeted member tests pass 4/4 and live source checks returned `information_observed` for all three pages. P5 remains **Partial**. Sources are linked in HANDOFF.md.
